Palacios Hernández\n    Director General del Instituto Nacional de Investigaciones\n                             Nucleares\n     Secretaría de Energía de los Estados Unidos Mexicanos\n        Viena, Austria, del 26 al 30 de septiembre de 2022\n\n\nExcelentísimo Señor Alessandro Cortese, Presidente de la\nSexagésima Sexta Reunión de la Conferencia General del Organismo\nInternacional de Energía Atómica.\n\nSeñor Rafael Mariano Grossi. Director General del OIEA,\n\nDistinguidas Delegadas, Distinguidos Delegados.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nEn nombre del Gobierno de México y en representación de la Ing.\nNorma Rocío Nahle García, Secretaria de Energía, deseo felicitarle por\nsu designación como Presidente de la Sexagésima Sexta Reunión\nOrdinaria de la Conferencia General del Organismo Internacional de\nEnergía Atómica.\n\nMéxico desea resaltar la destacada la labor y liderazgo del Director\nRafael Mariano Grossi, al frente del OIEA, para garantizar el\ncumplimiento de los objetivos del Organismo, en medio de\ncircunstancias excepcionalmente difíciles, tras dos años de pandemia\nprovocada por el virus SARS-CoV-2, y sus devastadoras consecuencias\nen los diferentes ámbitos de la sociedad.\n\nAsimismo, mi país elogia la importante labor que realiza el Director\nGrossi, al frente de la misión que se encuentra presente desde el 01 de\nseptiembre de este año, en la central nuclear Zaporizhzhya, en Ucrania;\npor lo que reiteramos nuestro firme apoyo a los esfuerzos del\nOrganismo para garantizar la seguridad de las instalaciones nucleares\nde Ucrania.\n\nMéxico reconoce la labor del OIEA en el cumplimiento de los\ncompromisos internacionales en materia de la promoción de la no\nproliferación nuclear, y el uso pacífico de la ciencia y tecnologías\nnucleares. En este sentido mi Delegación respalda las actividades de\nSalvaguardias del OIEA, y apoya el ejercicio constante del\nfortalecimiento de la Seguridad Física Nuclear, así como de la\nSeguridad Nuclear y Radiológica.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nMéxico reconoce que la utilización de tecnología nuclear para atender\nalgunas de sus prioridades en materia de desarrollo, en las áreas de\nsalud, alimentación, agricultura, agua, ambiente, y aplicaciones\nenergéticas, contribuye al crecimiento de mi país. Es por ello que\nactualmente en el Programa de Cooperación Técnica, México participa\nactivamente en la ejecución de 15 proyectos nacionales, y 53 proyectos\nregionales ARCAL, No ARCAL, e interregionales.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nMéxico reconoce el éxito de la Séptima Reunión de las Partes\nContratantes, como foro para el intercambio constructivo de puntos de\nvista, y aprendizaje sobre soluciones a problemas de seguridad\ncomunes e individuales, que conduzcan a la identificación de medidas\nencaminadas al logro y mantenimiento de un alto nivel de la seguridad\nnuclear a nivel mundial, a través de una revisión por pares efectiva,\ncabal y transparente.\n\nMéxico agradece al Organismo Internacional de Energía Atómica, por\nla redesignación del Instituto Nacional de Investigaciones Nucleares,\ncomo Centro Colaborador del OIEA para la Seguridad Operacional,\nMantenimiento y Mejoramiento de Instalaciones de Irradiación Gamma,\npor un nuevo período de 2022 a 2026, nos sentimos honrados por tal\ndistinción, y comprometidos con el logro de nuevos resultados tan\nfructíferos, como lo fueron en el primer ciclo de esta designación.\n\nSeñor Presidente,", "excerpt_sha256": "d84826b7363c01ba651edd227609145fe3e3a05a2c6513c1b4e9d37db54679be", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0031-E002", "source_id": "SRC-2671c8150bc71422", "url": "https://www.iaea.org/sites/default/files/22/09/mexico-gc66.pdf", "title": "México en el Debate General de la 66ª Conferencia General del OIEA", "publisher": "International Atomic Energy Agency", "raw_sha256": "5a55e9c53b714d6365bb41e10bed1f448d69bd1d615b972073aa6c618289ee24", "fact_summary": "México apoyó la revisión por pares transparente en seguridad nuclear; el regulador nuclear asumió la presidencia regional del FORO por un año; se renovó por 30 años la licencia de la Unidad 2 de Laguna Verde desde 2025, bajo el marco regulatorio aplicable y estándares internacionales de seguridad.", "question_ids": ["1B.3", "1B.7", "8B.2", "8B.6"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "La licencia y las funciones institucionales fueron anunciadas en 2022; la vigencia principal de la renovación comienza después del período 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "Las decisiones se atribuyen a la Secretaría de Energía y al organismo regulador mexicano, dentro del gobierno de AMLO.", "limitations": ["El documento es una declaración oficial y no aporta auditoría independiente ni evidencia de desempeño operacional posterior."], "start_unit": 2, "end_unit": 4, "locator": "page 2 through page 4", "exact_excerpt": "México reconoce la labor del OIEA en el cumplimiento de los\ncompromisos internacionales en materia de la promoción de la no\nproliferación nuclear, y el uso pacífico de la ciencia y tecnologías\nnucleares. En este sentido mi Delegación respalda las actividades de\nSalvaguardias del OIEA, y apoya el ejercicio constante del\nfortalecimiento de la Seguridad Física Nuclear, así como de la\nSeguridad Nuclear y Radiológica.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nMéxico reconoce que la utilización de tecnología nuclear para atender\nalgunas de sus prioridades en materia de desarrollo, en las áreas de\nsalud, alimentación, agricultura, agua, ambiente, y aplicaciones\nenergéticas, contribuye al crecimiento de mi país. Es por ello que\nactualmente en el Programa de Cooperación Técnica, México participa\nactivamente en la ejecución de 15 proyectos nacionales, y 53 proyectos\nregionales ARCAL, No ARCAL, e interregionales.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nMéxico reconoce el éxito de la Séptima Reunión de las Partes\nContratantes, como foro para el intercambio constructivo de puntos de\nvista, y aprendizaje sobre soluciones a problemas de seguridad\ncomunes e individuales, que conduzcan a la identificación de medidas\nencaminadas al logro y mantenimiento de un alto nivel de la seguridad\nnuclear a nivel mundial, a través de una revisión por pares efectiva,\ncabal y transparente.\n\nMéxico agradece al Organismo Internacional de Energía Atómica, por\nla redesignación del Instituto Nacional de Investigaciones Nucleares,\ncomo Centro Colaborador del OIEA para la Seguridad Operacional,\nMantenimiento y Mejoramiento de Instalaciones de Irradiación Gamma,\npor un nuevo período de 2022 a 2026, nos sentimos honrados por tal\ndistinción, y comprometidos con el logro de nuevos resultados tan\nfructíferos, como lo fueron en el primer ciclo de esta designación.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nMi país, quiere hacer una especial mención por el 25 aniversario del\nForo Iberoamericano de Organismos Reguladores Radiológicos y\nNucleares, destacando su inestimable labor, encaminada a la\npromoción y fortalecimiento de la seguridad nuclear, radiológica y física\nal más alto nivel en la región iberoamericana.\n\nEn este evento México a través de su Organismo Regulador, la\nComisión Nacional de Seguridad Nuclear y Salvaguardias, asumió la\npresidencia del FORO, por el periodo de un año, nos sentimos honrados\npor tan alta distinción, al tiempo que elogiamos la publicación del\n“Programa Técnico 1997-2022: 25 años del FORO”, que compila los\nresultados de los proyectos y actividades finalizadas y en desarrollo\ndesde su fundación en 1997.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nEs un gran placer poder comunicarle que, con base al compromiso con\nel ambiente, la seguridad nuclear y la soberanía energética, México\nreconociendo el papel sustancial que la Central Nucleoeléctrica Laguna\nVerde, representa para la generación de electricidad en nuestro país; el\n25 de agosto del presente año, la Secretaria de Energía, Ing. Norma\nRocío Nahle García, autorizó la renovación de la licencia de operación\na la Unidad 2 de la Central Nucleoeléctrica Laguna Verde, por un\nperiodo de 30 años más, a partir del 12 de abril de 2025, al 10 de abril\nde 2055.\n\nEn este sentido, la Central Nucleoeléctrica Laguna Verde, se mantiene\na la vanguardia en la producción de energías limpias del país, como\ngarante del sistema eléctrico nacional, bajo los más altos estándares\ninternacionales de seguridad, en conformidad con el marco regulador\naplicable, garantizando siempre su operación segura y confiable.\n\nSeñor Presidente,\n\nDeseo referirme ahora, al tema del Foro Científico de este año, que lleva\npor nombre “Rayos de Esperanza: Tratamiento Oncológico para\nTodos”.\n\nMéxico reconoce que la iniciativa “Rayos de Esperanza” del Organismo,\nayudará a incrementar las capacidades existentes de los países\n\nMiembros, en la lucha contra el cáncer, y será un apoyo sustancial\ncoadyuvante en el logro de las metas establecidas en la Agenda 2030\nde las Naciones Unidas para el Desarrollo Sostenible, a fin de reducir la\nmortalidad por dicha enfermedad.\n\nSeñor Presidente, Señor Director General, Distinguidos Delegados,\n\nMéxico desea expresar su firme determinación en contribuir al\ncumplimiento del mandato del OIEA, a través de la estrecha y eficaz\ncolaboración que se ha llevado a cabo desde hace más de 64 años, en\npro del bienestar nacional, así como de la comunidad internacional.\n\nPara el gobierno de mi país es prioritario garantizar el ejercicio igualitario\nde los derechos de todas y todos los ciudadanos, sin privilegios, y sin\ndistinciones. En este sentido la cooperación con el OIEA, ha permitido\nque cada vez sean más los mexicanos que se beneficien, a través de la\nutilización de las tecnologías nucleares, en condiciones seguras,\nprincipalmente en los temas de generación de energía eléctrica,\nradioterapia y radiofármacos en la lucha contra el cáncer, así como en\nla mayor calidad de los alimentos, a través de los usos de la irradiación.\n\nMéxico refrenda su papel cómo un actor socialmente responsable,\ncomprometido con la implementación, difusión y búsqueda continua de\naplicaciones pacíficas de la energía nuclear, en pro del bienestar de las\npresentes y futuras generaciones, con el propósito de que nuestra\nsociedad sea más equitativa.\n\n\nMuchas gracias.", "excerpt_sha256": "e08d5cd8358f8d49aac8beb123144b89f21325943bf548bb3b9101eb93bec9ea", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0038-E002", "source_id": "SRC-bd1e2d5a8f2af3bf", "url": "https://www-news.iaea.org/ErfView.aspx?mId=5a43e799-5249-49dd-b649-c5ac897a1dab", "title": "Stolen cat-3 source in Mexico", "publisher": "International Atomic Energy Agency Incident and Emergency Centre", "raw_sha256": "ad7c8cd7b243b344010f371467ee5eb1df7fcdf4ce42cc636fbdd46be2da48a7", "fact_summary": "On 18 October 2023, organized crime stole a radiography camera and vehicle in Ciudad del Carmen, Tabasco; the camera contained a Category 3 Ir-192 radioactive source. The IAEA rated the event INES 1, recorded degradation of defence in depth, and reported no public, worker, or environmental overexposure.", "question_ids": ["1B.3"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The incident and regulatory response occurred in March 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The report identifies Mexican regulatory and law-enforcement actions but does not attribute decisions personally to López Obrador.", "limitations": ["The supplied report concerns one radiological-security incident and does not establish broader national safeguards, funding, inspection frequency, or long-term correction."], "start_unit": 1, "end_unit": 3, "locator": "extracted HTML block 1 through extracted HTML block 3", "exact_excerpt": "NEWS\nSkip to content\nSkip to navigation\nYou are not signed in.\nSign In\nRegister\nIAEA\nNUCLEUS\nHome\nEvents\nThe INES Scale\nAbout News\nMissing Dangerous Source at Salamanca, Guanajuato, Mexico.\nPrint View\nPosted on: 30 March 2023\nEvent Date:\n22 March 2023\nEvent Type:\nRadiation Source\nEvent Location:\nMexico, Salamanca, Guanajuato, Mexico\nINES Rating:\n1 (Final)\nOn March 22, 2023 at approximately 11:00 a.m. (UTC-6), the theft of four industrial radiography devices with the following characteristics was reported to the CNSNS offices:\n1. Brand: AEA Technology, model: DELTA 880, Serial No. D2810, which houses a radioactive source of Ir-192 with serial number 083H with a current activity of 7.61 Ci.\n\n2. Brand: QSA Global Inc, model DELTA 880, Serial No. D5824, which houses a radioactive source of Ir-192 with serial number 370G with a current activity of 0.11 Ci.\n3. Brand: Source Production & Equipment Co, Model: SPEC-150, Serial No. 2173, which houses a radioactive source of Ir-192 with serial number C0052 with a current activity of 35.64 Ci.\n4. Brand: Industrial Nuclear Co., model IR100, serial No. 4829, which houses a radioactive source of Ir-192 with serial number TT3561 with a current activity of 1.14 Ci.\nThe theft took place on March 22, 2023 at approximately 3:00 a.m. (UTC-6), when armed persons entered the licensee’s facility located in Pánfilo Nájera 205, Col. Lázaro Cardenas, Salamanca, Guanajuato, Mexico. They extracted the four sources and a van.\nOn Tuesday March the 28th, 2023 at approximately 17:30 (UTC-6), The devices were found near to Salamanca Guanajuato due to an anonymous call. The devices were verified by the licensee and all radiological risk was discarded. The devices were recovered and returned to the licensee who took them to its facility. Search for the perpetrators is underway.\nOn Wednesday March the 29th 2023, a group of inspectors of the Regulatory Authority was sent to Salamanca, Guanajuato in order to verify that the devices were safe and the licensee facility had fixed and improved the security systems. At approximately 17:33 (UTC-6), the inspectors had given their approval to the new security measures placed in the facility.\nThe actions taken were: a) The theft has been reported to the law enforcement agencies; b) The National Regulatory body has notified the National Civil Protection Agency; c) Search for the missing industrial radiography devices is underway; d) A search team of the Regulatory Authority has been dispatched to the site in order to help and advice the responders; e) The Regulatory Authority personnel gave theoretical training to local first responders on radiological issues; f) The Licensee filed a legal complaint with the Prosecutor´s Office; g) The devices have been recovered. Radiological risk has been discarded and, e) The regulatory authority has inspected the improved security measures of the facility and found them satisfactory.\nINES Rating:\n1 - Anomaly (Final)\nas per 30 March 2023\nImpact on people and the environment\nRelease beyond authorized limits?\nNo\nOverexposure of a member of the public?\nNo\nOverexposure of a worker?\nNo\nImpact on the radiological barriers and controls at facilities\nContamination spread within the facility?\nNo\nDamage to radiological barriers (incl. fuel damage) within the facility?\nNo\nDegradation of Defence In-Depth\nYes\nOther information\n\nPerson injured physically or casualty?\nNo\nIs there a continuing problem?\nNo\nContact Person for Further Information\nVictor Gonzalez\nComision Nacional de Seguridad Nuclear y Salvaguardias\nvmgonzalez@cnsns.gob.mx\nOther Posts on this Event\n3/22/2023 -\nMissing Dangerous Source at Salamanca, Guanajuato, Mexico.\nSite Sponsors\nThis site is hosted by the International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) and co-sponsored by the Nuclear Energy Agency of the Organization of Economic Cooperation Development (OECD/NEA) and World Association of Nuclear Operators (WANO).\nDisclaimer\nThis site is hosted by the IAEA, however, event reports are filed and updated by authorities in participating countries, which remain responsible for all related content.\nRead more >\nRSS Feed\nVersion 7.1.9237, Copyright ©2010-2026 International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA). "exact_excerpt": "STATEMENT BY THE\nPERMANENT REPRESENTATIVE OF MEXICO TO THE UN,\nAMBASSADOR JUAN RAMÓN DE LA FUENTE\nAT THE GENERAL DEBATE OF THE\nTENTH REVIEW CONFERENCE OF THE\nTREATY ON THE NON-PROLIFERATION OF\nNUCLEAR WEAPONS\n- UNOFFICIAL TRANSLATION-\nNew York, 2 August 2022.\nMr. President,\nMexico is pleased that this Conference is being held at last, and that\nit is you who leads its endeavors. We recognize your tireless work\nto achieve this, in complex circumstances, and we reiterate our\nsupport for bringing the Conference to a successful conclusion.\nI would like to begin my statement by briefly recalling some\nhistorical context that comes to mind. After the so-called missile\ncrisis in 1962, but even from the beginning of the Cold War, Mexico\ncould have joined the United States nuclear deterrence system. We\nwere, besides being neighbors, a State party to the Inter-American\nTreaty of Reciprocal Assistance of 1948 (Treaty of Rio), the only\ndefensive pact of the Americas.\nBut Mexico chose to structure the defense of its territory and the\nsecurity of its population, based on international law and, above all,\non the basis of the Charter of the United Nations. The International\nCourt of Justice unequivocally established in 1996 that the use of\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/\n\nnuclear weapons \"would generally be contrary to international\nlaw\", something my country had always maintained.\nPrecisely for this reason, Mexico took the initiative to create the first\nnuclear-weapon-free zone in Latin America and the Caribbean,\nthrough the Treaty of Tlatelolco of 1967. Prior to the NPT, and for\nmore than 70 years, my country has made countless contributions\nto the international disarmament regime.\nThe most recent of these is the 2017 Treaty on the Prohibition of\nNuclear Weapons (TPNW), logical culmination of international law\nand of the conviction of the General Assembly regarding the\n\"inherently immoral\" nature of nuclear weapons (res. A/76/25). This\nTreaty is increasingly becoming universal and Mexico will have the\nhonor of chairing its Second Meeting of States Parties in 2023.\nWe come to this Conference today to reaffirm our conviction that\nthe NPT is the cornerstone of the nonproliferation and\ndisarmament regime. Therefore, we do not, come here to barter our\nsupport for the NPT. Its provisions must be fully implemented, in a\nbalanced manner, and without conditions, by all its parties. The so-\ncalled grand bargain should not be reinterpreted, nor distorted.\nIt is a fact that the objectives of the NPT on nonproliferation, as well\nas those related to the peaceful uses of atomic energy, have been\nlargely met. With very few exceptions, the Non-nuclear weapons\nStates have fulfilled our part of that Great Negotiation. Some, such\nas Mexico, have even made additional and complementary\nvoluntary commitments to the NPT, such as the Additional Protocol\nof the International Atomic Energy Agency and those derived from\nexport control regimes and, of course, the Ban Treaty, the TPNW.\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/\n\nMuch remains to be done to further strengthen the nuclear\nnonproliferation regime, such as achieving the universality of the\nNPT, strengthening the implementation of the safeguards system,\nand ensuring that more countries benefit from atomic energy for\npeaceful purposes, as well as guaranteeing the safety and security\nof nuclear facilities. We hope that this Conference will take concrete\nsteps in these directions.\nHowever, the great pending issue of the NPT continues to be the\ndisarmament obligations contained in its article 6. Around 13,000\nnuclear weapons remain in the arsenals of the Nuclear Weapon\nStates, who justify their existence in Cold War military doctrines:\nThis undermines the unequivocal undertaking to eliminate nuclear\nweapons assumed by Nuclear Weapons States at the 2000 Review\nConference.\nMexico firmly rejects any argument that seeks to justify the\ncontinued existence of nuclear weapons and the threat of their use.\nTherefore, we expect, at a minimum, that the Nuclear Weapons\nStates reaffirm their commitment to the implementation of their\nobligations under the NPT, and report to the Conference on their\nprogress within deadlines and benchmarks, in order to build\nconfidence among all States Parties.\nThis Conference, colleagues, is taking place in the context of the\narmed conflict in Ukraine in which the Russian Federation has\nplaced its nuclear systems on high operational alert. This increases\nthe dangers associated with their intentional or accidental use.\nThis is even less comprehensible since the Nuclear Weapon States,\nin preparation for this Conference, had rightly reaffirmed on 3\nJanuary of this year, that a nuclear war cannot be fought, since it\ncannot be won.\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/\n\nThe core concern about the humanitarian consequences of the use\nof nuclear weapons is enshrined in the Treaty of Tlatelolco, the NPT\nand the Comprehensive Nuclear Tests Ban Treaty, and is the\nrationale for the TPNW. It is not, therefore, a concept alien to the\nNPT and, as such, should be recognized by the Review Conference,\nas it did in 2010.\nMr. President,\nMexico will do everything in its power to ensure that this\nConference concludes with a final agreement that includes the\nreview of the implementation of the NPT, as well as prospective\nsteps that, based on the agreements and obligations previously\nadopted, contributes to a safer and more peaceful world, based on\ninternational law and not on the state of terror of nuclear weapons.\nThank you very much.\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/", "excerpt_sha256": "e07001215f9097d5df980421878d6d7dbfbe87549456758ab92ec617e2832dc4", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0043-E004", "source_id": "SRC-0b1b38fdcb022f70", "url": "https://estatements.un.org/estatements/11.0010/20231017100000000/J9YeTo40may2/rAMad8VtILfC_m.pdf", "title": "Mexico statement rejecting weapons of mass destruction", "publisher": "United Nations", "raw_sha256": "153c6d875b6e58a39b80e11d23ada9a4a466dbcfcb7ed54e7b30a21d525136f3", "fact_summary": "In Mexico's October 2023 UN First Committee statement, the government rejected all weapons of mass destruction and their use under any circumstances, urged universalization of the Chemical Weapons Convention, supported stronger verification for the Biological Weapons Convention, highlighted catastrophic dual-use risks in synthetic biology, biotechnology, biogenetics, and research, and called for stronger biological-material controls and analysis of emerging technologies through the Security Council 1540 Committee.", "question_ids": ["1B.1", "1B.10", "1B.2", "1B.4", "1B.6", "1B.9"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Directly dated to 17 October 2023 and therefore fits the target year.", "ruler_attribution": "The statement is formally delivered by Mexico; Reiteramos\nque éstas no deben usarse por ningún actor, en ningún lugar y bajo ninguna\ncircunstancia.\nSeñor Presidente:\nEn el marco de la Convención sobre Armas Químicas (CAQ), México se\ncongratula por que se ha alcanzado la destrucción completa de todos los\narsenales declarados por los Estados Parte.\nEl pasado 7 de julio de 2023, concluyó el proceso de destrucción irreversible\ndel arsenal químico de Estados Unidos. No se trata de un asunto menor ya\nque supone un paso firme en la dirección correcta en la prohibición y\neliminación de una categoría completa de armas de destrucción en masa,\ny hacia el desarme general y completo.\nNo obstante, cuatro Estados todavía no son Parte de la Convención, por lo\nque México exhorta a dichos países a que se sumen plenamente a ella para\nasí cumplir con la aspiración de su universalización.\nAsimismo, México considera que en esta nueva fase después de la\ndestrucción del arsenal estadounidense, es sumamente relevante definir\nnuevas oportunidades estratégicas para la Organización para la Prohibición\nde las Armas Químicas (OPAQ). Estamos frente a una gran oportunidad,\nespecialmente frente a retos derivados de los avances de la ciencia y\ntecnologías, incluyendo aquellas de uso dual.\nSeñor Presidente:\nSi bien México reconoce los avances alcanzados en la novena conferencia\nde revisión de la Convención sobre Armas Bacteriológicas (CABT), en\nespecial el establecimiento del grupo de trabajo intersesional, todavía no\nestamos cerca del establecimiento de un mecanismo de verificación formal.\n1\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/\n\nEn consecuencia, hacemos eco del llamado de la Alta Representante\nNakamitsu para que se tomen pasos decisivos hacia un régimen de\nverificación que dé seguridad sobre el cumplimiento de todas las\ndisposiciones de la Convención.\nEsto es especialmente necesario dado el potencial de nuevas tecnologías\nen materia de biología sintética, biotecnología y biogenética, así como de la\ninvestigación científica, para asegurar el desarrollo de los países y el\ncumplimiento de los Objetivos de Desarrollo Sostenible, particularmente\npor los avances en la salud, la agricultura y la industria, entre otros. No\nobstante, esta misma tecnología tiene un uso dual, lo cual puede dar pie a\nriesgos con un impacto global catastrófico.\nSeñor Presidente:\nFinalizo con una breve referencia al Comité 1540 del Consejo de Seguridad.\nMi país tuvo el privilegio de conducir los trabajos del Comité en 2021 y 2022,\nincluyendo el examen amplio de la resolución 1540 (2004). Algunas de las\nrecomendaciones se centran en la necesidad de mayor atención a los\ncontroles sobre materiales biológicos, donde se registra menos progreso.\nLlamamos a que en esta nueva década de vida del Comité 1540, se avance\ndecididamente en materia de controles biológicos y que dicho foro analice\na profundidad los impactos de las tecnologías emergentes.\nMuchas gracias.\nOOo\n2\nTwo United Nations Plaza, 28th floor, New York, NY 10017\nTel: (212) 752-0220 http://mision.sre.gob.mx/onu/", "excerpt_sha256": "8d52c36666e85a2610fe62d4f7b6a5ca505c8f6c49eccef81c2f65d3a4af2c42", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0044-E001", "source_id": "SRC-99d96e86c8b3085c", "url": "https://treaties.un.org/Pages/ViewDetails.aspx?chapter=26&clang=_en&mtdsg_no=XXVI-9&src=IND", "title": "Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons: status of treaties", "publisher": "United Nations Treaty Collection", "raw_sha256": "41ea53aa9a46801c5d1ac975a2f6a538e54eef373cbae1ec43522d5c2ef2dff0", "fact_summary": "Mexico was a signatory to the Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons from 20 September 2017 and ratified it on 16 January 2018; the treaty was in force during 2023.", "question_ids": ["1B.10", "1B.4"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Directly applicable to 2023 treaty status; the underlying decisions predate the period.", "ruler_attribution": "Mexico's treaty position was established during López Obrador's presidency.", "limitations": ["The source establishes treaty status, not implementation, funding, or enforcement measures during 2023."], "start_unit": 5, "end_unit": 12, "locator": "extracted HTML block 5 through extracted HTML block 12", "exact_excerpt": "3.\n4.\n5.\n6.\n7.\n8.\n9.\nSTATUS AS AT : 31-07-2026 09:15:29 EDT\nCHAPTER XXVI\nDISARMAMENT\n9. resource allocation, or measurable effects."], "start_unit": 1, "end_unit": 4, "locator": "page 1 through page 4", "exact_excerpt": "Statement by Mexico on behalf of the New Agenda Coalition at\nthe First Committee: General Debate\nNew York, 4 October 2023\nOctober 2023\nI have the honor to take the floor on behalf of the members of the New\nAgenda Coalition (NAC), Brazil, Egypt, Ireland, New Zealand, South\nAfrica, and my own country, Mexico. At the outset, the NAC\ncongratulates you on your assumption of your role as Chair of this\nyear’s First Committee. We assure you of the NAC’s full cooperation.\nMr. President,\nThis year, the NAC commemorates twenty-five years since the Dublin\nDeclaration on “A Nuclear Weapons-Free World: the Need for a New\nAgenda”, which established our initiative. Since 1998, the NAC has\nnever wavered in its advocacy of nuclear disarmament negotiations. In\nso doing, the NAC has emphasized the moral and ethical underpinnings\nof the fulfillment of disarmament obligations and commitments that all\nStates Parties to the NPT, in particular the Nuclear-Weapon States,\nmust abide by.\nWe recognize there have been certain accomplishments in nuclear\ndisarmament since the year of our founding. But the reality is that the\nachievement, let alone maintenance, of a world free of nuclear\nweapons, is still pending, and so must still be an urgent priority of the\ninternational community.\nWe note with deep concern, therefore, the profound global peace and\nsecurity challenges we are facing today. We are alarmed that recent\ninternational tensions, including within the context of the conflict in\nUkraine, have taken an increasing nuclear dimension, particularly with\nregard to threats to use nuclear weapons and increasingly strident\nnuclear rhetoric, which we unequivocally condemn. We underline the\ndangers this brings –occurring as it does against the backdrop of the\n1\n\nnuclear-weapon States and States under extended nuclear security\nguarantees giving increased prominence to maintaining and even\nincreasing the role of nuclear weapons in security doctrines, as well as\nthe placement of nuclear weapons on the territory of non-nuclear-\nweapon States. We have noted for some time the plans by the nuclear-\nweapon States to expand, modernize and qualitatively improve their\nnuclear arsenals. Alongside this, there are now disturbing signs that\nnuclear weapons stockpiles are increasing. Other negative\ndevelopments include the weakening of the disarmament and non-\nproliferation architecture, including the erosion or demise of existing\nnuclear arms control treaties and the continuing disregard of the need\nto strengthen negative security assurances.\nIn this context, we are dismayed at the successive failures of the two\nmost recent Review Conferences of the NPT. State Parties to the Treaty\nwere unable to agree actions that would strengthen the NPT regime by\nconsensus. The inability of the working group on further strengthening\nthe review process of the Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear\nWeapons to agree on substantive outcomes and recommendations was\nof further concern, and did not reflect the groundswell of support\nevident for this work.\nThe NAC reiterates that the existing nuclear disarmament-related\nobligations and commitments remain valid, including the unequivocal\nundertaking of the Nuclear-Weapon States to accomplish the total\nelimination of their nuclear arsenals leading to nuclear disarmament.\nAccordingly, NAC will continue to advocate for the implementation of\nconcrete, transparent, mutually reinforcing, verifiable and irreversible\nnuclear disarmament measures as part of the fulfilment of NPT\nobligations and commitments.\nWe continue to express deep concern at the catastrophic humanitarian\nconsequences that would result from any use of nuclear weapons.\nUltimately, humanitarian imperatives underpin nuclear disarmament\nand the urgency of achieving it, especially when new evidence, such as\nthe highly disproportionate and gendered impact of exposure to\n2\n\nionizing radiation for women and girls, amongst other effects, is taken\ninto account.\nIn 1995, the basis for the indefinite extension of the NPT was, and\nremains, “permanence with accountability”. It is critical that within the\ncurrent review cycle of the Treaty we collectively advance measures on\nthe agreements of the 1995, 2000 and 2010 Review Conferences on the\nNPT, in particular, the “13 steps” on nuclear disarmament.\nWe urge the Nuclear Weapon States to implement all their nuclear\ndisarmament obligations and commitments, in a manner that\nstrengthens accountability and enables all States parties to regularly\nmonitor progress, thereby enhancing transparency and increasing\nmutual confidence. This would also facilitate the evidence-based\nevaluation of progress towards the full implementation of article VI and\nnuclear disarmament commitments.\nMeasures to this end should include improved reporting by the nuclear-\nweapon States, ideally through a standard detailed format with concrete\nand detailed information concerning implementation of their\nobligations and commitments. It should also include details on their\nplans related to the modernization of nuclear weapons; their nuclear\ncapabilities including quantity, type and status of nuclear warheads, as\nwell as delivery vehicles; doctrinal issues; risk reduction measures; de-\nalerting measures; quantity of fissile material; and the number and type\nof weapons and delivery systems they disarmed. It should also other\ntools such as a set of benchmarks, timelines and/or similar criteria, in\norder to ensure and facilitate the objective evaluation of progress.\nMr. President,\nOne highlight is the consolidation of the Treaty on the Prohibition of\nNuclear Weapons (TPNW) regime, with positive steps to universalize\nthe TPNW and implement its action plan adopted last year in Vienna.\n3\n\nWe commend those states that recognize the TPNW for what it is – as\nan “effective measure” for nuclear disarmament, one fully consistent\nwith and complementing the NPT. We further encourage all States to\nparticipate in its Second Meeting of State Parties, whether as State\nParties or Observers.\nWe are also encouraged by the successful organization in 2019, 2021\nand 2022 by the Secretary General of the UN, in accordance with its\ndecision 73/546 of 22 December 2018, of the sessions of a conference\naimed at elaborating a treaty on the establishment of a Middle East zone\nfree of nuclear weapons and all other weapons of mass destruction, on\nthe basis of arrangements freely arrived at by the States of the region.\nThe NAC encourages all concerned parties referred in the decision to\nactively engage in the 2023 and ensuing sessions of the Conference.\nMr. President,\nThe NPT is the cornerstone of the nuclear disarmament and non-\nproliferation regime. But there is a deficit growing in terms of\nimplementation of the Article 6 obligation and nuclear disarmament-\nrelated commitments by the Nuclear Weapon States. To preserve the\ncredibility and strength of the Treaty, the Nuclear Weapon States must\nimplement their respective obligations and commitments in full and\nwithout continued delay and therefore redress the imbalance in\nimplementation with regard to Non-Nuclear Weapon States.\nAny presumption of indefinite possession of nuclear weapons runs\ncounter to the object and purpose of the Treaty, and any threat of use\nnuclear weapons, is contrary to the Charter of the UN and the multiple\nresolutions adopted each year by the General Assembly.\nI thank you.\noOo\n4", "excerpt_sha256": "6b5a3074c516e6c889caf660190f885898e287ba72de52fe85d7e099012723e6", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": ["The excerpt supports Mexico's October 2023 statement on behalf of the NAC, its condemnation of nuclear threats and rhetoric, proposed disarmament and NPT measures, and encouragement of TPNW participation."]}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0045-E013", "source_id": "SRC-114805f9ea1298a0", "url": "https://unoda-documents-library.s3.amazonaws.com/Treaty_on_the_Non-Proliferation_of_Nuclear_Weapons_-_Tenth_Review_Conference_%282022%29/Statement_by_Mexico_on_behalf_of_the_TPNW_State_Parties_and_Signatories.pdf", "title": "Statement by Mexico on behalf of TPNW states and signatories", "publisher": "UNODA", "raw_sha256": "fa9f9203e924595245691940855520c103d3f88fc18f994af029436dc421b6e0", "fact_summary": "Mexico delivered a joint statement on behalf of Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons parties and signatories, reaffirming commitment to the NPT, nuclear disarmament, the Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty, risk-reduction measures, verification, negative security assurances, and a fissile-material prohibition.", "question_ids": ["1B.1", "1B.2", "1B.4", "8B.1"], "polarity": "favorable", "period_fit": "Statement delivered August 17, 2022; Experts linked the weakness to austerity-related cancellation of cybersecurity equipment and technology, a general security deficiency, and too few cybersecurity officials and limited institutional capacity. López Obrador acknowledged the hack but, in the reported remarks, did not address the cybersecurity weakness or announce a remedy. The army had also received expanded state responsibilities, including customs and major infrastructure.", "question_ids": ["1B.10", "1B.3", "1B.7", "1B.9", "5B.6", "7B.2", "7B.7", "8B.10", "8B.3", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8", "8B.9"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "The breach occurred in September 2022; the exposed institutional weakness and reported lack of corrective response are relevant to the 2023 posture.", "ruler_attribution": "The article attributes the expansion of military responsibilities and the president's response to López Obrador; expert explanations of the cybersecurity failure are not proof of his personal intent.", "limitations": ["The article does not establish the complete budgetary or administrative causes of the breach.", "It reports no later 2023 remediation outcome."], "start_unit": 3, "end_unit": 4, "locator": "extracted HTML block 3 through extracted HTML block 4", "exact_excerpt": "Compartir:\nWhatsapp\nFacebook\nTwitter\nBluesky\nLinkedin\nCopiar enlace\nEl\nciberataque sufrido por el sistema de comunicación del Ejército\nha dejado expuestos más de cuatro millones de correos electrónicos, muchos con información confidencial, donde caben desde la reacción de los militares ante el enfrentamiento en 2019 con el poderoso cartel de Sinaloa hasta las múltiples enfermedades del presidente y de miembros de su familia. La mayor filtración de datos del Ejército mexicano, que se recuerda, ha revelado la omnipresencia de las Fuerzas Armadas en la vida nacional y ha dejado expuesta la debilidad del Ejército con una grave falla de seguridad digital.\nEl presidente Andrés Manuel López Obrador ha evitado hablar sobre la falta de ciberseguridad y las consecuencias que estas puedan tener en las próximas semanas. El mandatario ha depositado en el Ejército la responsabilidad de la seguridad nacional y le ha concedido en este sexenio algunas de las más importantes tareas del Estado, como la vigilancia de las aduanas o la construcción de obras civiles de gran calado,\nel Tren Maya o el aeropuerto Felipe Ángeles,\npor citar dos casos. El poder alcanzado por la Secretaría de la Defensa da la medida del impacto de esta filtración confidencial y evidencia la debilidad de las comunicaciones más delicadas del país.\nRápido, los expertos han puesto en cuestión la “frágil” política de ciberseguridad en México, exhibida por el grupo de\nhacktivistas\nGuacamaya, que ya abrió otros preocupantes agujeros en la información que custodian Fuerzas Armadas de países como Chile, Colombia o El Salvador. “Las\nmedidas de austeridad de este Gobierno\nhan cancelado muchas cosas, entre ellas la compra de equipo y tecnología para seguridad informática. El Ejército no tenía la protección debida, lo mismo que ocurrió en Pemex [en noviembre de 2019], que también fue víctima de estas filtraciones. Es una carencia general de seguridad”, afirma el analista de seguridad Guillermo Valdés.\nLas tesis de muchos especialistas insisten en la necesidad de redactar una ley de ciberseguridad. “Con la austeridad sería complicado crear una nueva burocracia, pero quizá [esta tarea] la podría asumir alguna otra instancia, quizá el Centro Nacional de Inteligencia (CNI)”, dice Gerardo Rodríguez, director académico del departamento de Relaciones Internacionales y Ciencias Políticas de la Universidad de las Américas- Puebla. El asunto está presente en las conversaciones diplomáticas: “Uno de los puntos del acuerdo del\nEntendimiento Bicentenario entre México y Estados Unidos\ntrataba sobre aumentar la cooperación en materia de ciberseguridad. Y Antony Blinken, [secretario de Estado de Estados Unidos] insistió en ese asunto recientemente”, asegura Rodríguez. “Por más que México tenga una política de no intervención en otras soberanías, no somos amigos del mundo”, añade el especialista. Y advierte: “Hoy son hacktivistas, pero mañana podrían ser terroristas o potencias extranjeras” las que se cuelen hasta la cocina en los asuntos más estratégicos de México.\nEl Gobierno disolvió al Estado Mayor Presidencial, una institución de origen militar, pero que mantenía su independencia, y ahora está subsumida en la Secretaría de Defensa. “Ese Estado Mayor es quien debería disponer de las informaciones relacionadas con la vida del presidente y de su familia que han salido a la luz, pero el presidente lo desapareció y lo fundió con el Ejército”, dice Valdés. La medida se revela ahora en todo su riesgo, al parecer de este especialista, que fue jefe del servicio de inteligencia mexicano entre 2007 y 2011. “Esa información podría ser usada para chantajear al presidente, por ejemplo. Y si los militares tuvieran tentaciones golpistas, mejor sería que no dispusieran de esos datos. El Estado Mayor debería ser una rama separada y fiel a la Presidencia”, sugiere.\nValdés cree que el Ejército dispone hoy de más información de la que cabría suponer, debido, asegura, a la estrecha relación que ha forjado el presidente y su familia con los militares y la cantidad de tareas cruciales que les han asignado. “Las revelaciones de hoy confirman hasta qué punto el presidente ha permitido al Ejército estar cerca de él y de las decisiones de poder”.\nLa colosal filtración de correos electrónicos llega en un momento político que la desnuda de inocencia. Valdés no cree que sea casualidad el tiempo elegido para hacer públicas estas comunicaciones, es decir, para evidenciar la debilidad el sistema de ciberseguridad mexicano. “Justo ahora se está discutiendo\nla fuerte presencia y el papel que juega el Ejército en la vida pública\n\nde país. Parece claro que se quiere mostrar la debilidad de la Institución militar. Es como decir: ‘cuidado con el Ejército y toda la información que posee”. Mucha, para tanta vulnerabilidad.\nValdés lamenta que este Gobierno haya “degradado al CNI al situarlo bajo el mando de la Secretaría de Seguridad Pública, como si la seguridad nacional fuera un tema menor”. Explica que alrededor de 2008, la Inteligencia mexicana, el entonces CISEN, “coordinó un proyecto de ciberseguridad donde participaba todo el gabinete de seguridad, pero el proyecto quedó cancelado hace años. El asunto se estancó”, asegura. “Al presidente no le ha importado la ciberseguridad”, critica. “Es un tema central que debería atender el CNI de forma seria”.\nEn buena medida, coincide con estas opiniones Guillermo Rodríguez, aunque cree que “la supuesta falta de interés del presidente no quiere decir que no exista en otras instituciones del Estado, como Defensa, Marina, el CNI o la Inteligencia Científica. El problema es que son pocos los funcionarios dedicados a la ciberseguridad, que rozan el centenar en la Agencia y deberían ser unos 300. México tiene capacidad para invertir en esta área. De lo contrario, seguiremos perdiendo importancia como potencia media”, señala Rodríguez. Explica que la Guardia Nacional heredó una estructura profesional en esta materia, pero pequeña, que debería crecer. “No podemos dejar de lado estas amenazas”.\nRodríguez pone de ejemplo lo ocurrido en Estonia, que sufrió su primer ciberataque, “patrocinado por Moscú”, hace más de 15 años y tomó la decisión de crear una infraestructura fuerte de ciberseguridad y ciberdefensa. “Hoy está en los niveles más altos en esa materia”.\nMéxico ha vivido este jueves esa desagradable experiencia, que puede airear los problemas más íntimos de la familia presidencial, algunos sin interés ciudadano alguno, quizá ni estratégico, solo alimento para el morbo. Interesa desde luego la salud de presidente y López Obrador no ha tenido inconveniente en reconocer, desde punto y hora, que está “enfermo”. Tampoco era la gran revelación, habida cuenta de que se conocen sus problemas coronarios, que ya le causaron un infarto en 2013 y\nque le someten a cuidados especiales continuos.\nNada ha mencionado de la debilidad del sistema de ciberseguridad mexicano ni de posibles intenciones de solventarlo.\nSuscríbase aquí\na la\nnewsletter\nde EL PAÍS México y reciba todas las claves informativas de la actualidad de este país\nSobre la firma\nCarmen Morán Breña\nVer biografía\nTrabaja en EL PAÍS desde 1997, donde ha sido jefa de sección en Sociedad, Nacional y Cultura. In Mexico, fundamental advances here would require\ndifficult changes to foundational legislation underpinning government organization (“Parastatal\nEntities Law”). As such, the FSAP team also suggests considering the following issues:\n\n•   Progress on strengthening the independence and legal protection of supervisors is\n    needed. Regulatory and supervisory agencies do not have budget autonomy (except Banxico)\n    with their budgets determined by SHCP. The head of CNBV is appointed by the finance minister\n    for an undefined term and can be removed from office for reasons not specified in law. The law\n    does not protect supervisors adequately while discharging their duties in good faith. While\n    lawsuits against supervisors have not been frequent, the lack of legal protection undermines\n    conditions for effective supervision. This protection is critical in the Mexican context, where each\n    supervisory conduct and measure should be described in the law.\n\n•   Filling the vacancies for independent members on the IPAB Board should be given the\n    highest priority. Four independent members, appointed by the president, hold a majority on\n    the IPAB Board. Three of these positions have been vacant for some time and need to be filled\n    swiftly. The safeguards for the autonomy of IPAB’s executive management should be strengthened\n    by introducing a set term of appointment and more objective grounds for dismissal.\n\n37. The organizational structure and resource needs of individual agencies would benefit\nfrom updated evaluation. The urgency is highlighted by the recent significant reduction in their\noperating budget (except Banxico), resulting in staff turnover and loss of experience. The FSAP\nrecommends a strategic review of the organizational structure and resources of relevant agencies to\nmake necessary adjustments to address existing and emerging challenges. The FSAP welcomes the\nongoing evaluation in IPAB as a good example.\n\n38. Interagency collaboration could also be further enhanced especially on emerging risks.\nThe MFAs could regularly conduct table-top crisis simulation exercises with a range of extreme but\nplausible scenarios, including fast-fail resolutions of systemic and medium-size banks, their\nconcurrent failure, and cyber crises. The authorities could also explore options to pool resources and\nexpertise across institutions to mitigate resource constraints and develop new analysis of cyber and\nclimate-related risks.\n\n39. New risks have emerged on the Financial Market Infrastructure (FMI) landscape since\nthe 2016 FSAP, and it is important to enhance the oversight of the Interbank Electronic\nPayment System (SPEI). Cyber risk has intensified worldwide. Other challenges include the\nemergent use of instant payments, the possible interaction with distributed ledger technology, and\nstablecoin arrangements. This increasingly complex risk landscape accentuates the need for\neffective, continuous FMI oversight. A full assessment of the FMI oversight is not included in the\nscope of the 2022 FSAP, but a focused review of the oversight function of the SPEI payment system\nfinds that Banxico could enhance the SPEI oversight approach by establishing a formal oversight\nfunction independent from the SPEI operators and addressing any gaps identified through the\nCPMI-IOSCO PFMI implementation monitoring exercise (see Box 1).\n\n\n                                                                   INTERNATIONAL MONETARY FUND       33\n\nMEXICO\n\n\n\n               Box 1. Oversight of the Interbank Electronic Payment System (SPEI)\n Mexico’s FMIs have previously been assessed by CPMI-IOSCO and past FSAPs, and a full assessment\n of these is not included in the scope of the 2022 FSAP. According to the CPMI-IOSCO Level 1 assessment\n and update in July 2018, measures were fully implemented for adopting legislation, regulations, and policies\n for the 24 Principles for Financial Market Infrastructures (PFMI) and four of the five authorities’\n responsibilities. The authorities observed or broadly observed most of the responsibilities, and partly\n observed the application of the principles for FMIs for central counterparties, as of November 2015. The\n systemically important payment system (SPEI) was also assessed during the 2016 and 2006 FSAPs in detail.\n\n However, as new risks have emerged on the FMI landscape, it is important to enhance the oversight\n of the SPEI payment system relative to the PFMI and cybersecurity. While the SPEI operators in Banxico\n have taken significant steps in strengthening the cyber resilience of the SPEI, there are improvements that\n can be made to strengthen the overall oversight approach, including for cybersecurity, to bring it in line with\n CPMI-IOSCO’s expectations, as set out in its Responsibilities A-E.\n\n •   Banxico could establish a more thorough oversight function for the SPEI system. It could conduct\n     risk assessments against the PFMIs applicable to payment systems and its supplemental guidance on a\n     continuous basis. The oversight function would benefit from having sufficient resources and\n     independence from the SPEI operators. Banxico could establish a structured oversight approach and\n     methodology after reviewing oversight models and approaches by other central banks to help inform\n     their own optimal model.\n\n •   Banxico could improve transparency. Banxico disclosed its responses to the CPSS-IOSCO Disclosure\n     framework for FMIs in March 2016. However, it should, at a minimum, review its responses to the\n     disclosure framework and disclose publicly its responses every two years to ensure continued accuracy\n     and usefulness.\n\n •   Banxico could address the gaps identified through the CPMI-IOSCO PFMI implementation\n     monitoring exercise. This specifically relates to the implementation by Banxico of a timebound work\n     plan for the SPEI payment system to fully observe all the principles of the PFMI.\n\n •   Banxico could conduct periodic self-assessments of its observance of the responsibilities of the\n     PFMI, i.e., Responsibilities A-E. It should aim to self-assess how effectively authorities fulfill their\n     responsibilities as regulators, supervisors, and overseers.\n\n\n\nFINANCIAL SECTOR OVERSIGHT\nA. Macroprudential Framework\n40. The authorities could develop and publish a macroprudential policy strategy.\nComplementing the Central Bank’s high-quality Financial Stability Report and other guidance issued\nby the CESF, the strategy can describe policy objectives, a list of instruments, decision-making\nprocesses, interagency coordination mechanisms, and the importance of retaining discretion when\ncalibrating instruments based on a set of quantitative indicators and expert judgement. International\nexperience shows that a formal strategy can boost the communication and traction of\nmacroprudential policies, improve accountability, counter potential inaction bias, enhance policy\n\n\n\n\n34   INTERNATIONAL MONETARY FUND\n\nMEXICO\n\n\n\ncoordination across regulatory and supervisory agencies, and strengthen credibility and\neffectiveness (IMF, 2014; CGFS, 2016). 11\n\n41. A rich set of macroprudential tools has helped build resilience. Capital and liquidity tools\nand D-SIB buffers were introduced in line with Basel requirements (Table 11). A core funding ratio in\nFX has been effective for banks to manage FX risks. Measures to reduce vulnerabilities from exposures\nto related parties or liquidity risks in investment funds (e.g., swing pricing, redemption fees) are in place.\n\n42. The authorities could consider expanding the toolkit and enhancing its time-varying\nuse. Plans for finalizing and publishing a guideline for the countercyclical capital buffer (CCyB) are\nwelcome and action at the current early stage of the financial cycle would be helpful. Indeed, with\nno evidence of credit and housing booms, introducing limits on loan-to-value and debt-service-to-\nincome ratios could be considered as a medium-term agenda to build the resilience of the financial\nsystem preemptively.\n\nB. Banking Regulation and Supervision\n43. The authorities have made considerable progress in strengthening banking regulation\nand supervision. CNBV has operationalized the ICAAP and the SRP (though ¶27 notes the need for\nenhancements), and the operational risk management framework has significantly improved.\nProspects for issuing amended regulations on large exposures in 2022 are important. The\namendment will include new definitions and limits for “common risk” and “related parties,” and it is\ncritical that they follow Basel III standards and best international practices (see Appendix Table 1 for\nthe summary compliance with Basel Core Principles).\n\n44. Some financial conglomerates operating in Mexico are outside CNBV’s consolidated\nsupervision. A financial conglomerate can voluntarily request SHCP for legal authorization to\noperate as a financial group in Mexico. While most financial conglomerates operate as authorized\nfinancial groups, seven have not requested the SHCP authorization at the time of the FSAP\nassessment and are not subject to the CNBV’s consolidated supervision.\n\n45. The legal and regulatory framework should be amended to enhance consolidated\nsupervision. The issuance of the 2014 Financial Groups Law (LRAF) was an important step toward a\nlegal framework for consolidated supervision, but it does not empower CNBV to impose prudential\nmeasures on financial conglomerates at the group level. 12 An amendment is needed to provide\nCNBV with powers to (i) apply a comprehensive set of prudential requirements on financial groups\nas a whole, (ii) enforce consolidated supervision on all de-facto financial conglomerates in Mexico,\nand (iii) impose discretionary powers to oversight all relevant entities of financial groups, including\nthose not declared during the authorization and those operating abroad.\n\n\n\n11\n     Good examples include the Czech Republic, Euro Area, Ireland, New Zealand, and South Africa.\n12\n  Each financial institution (e.g., bank, insurer, and pension fund) is supervised by a separate regulatory and\nsupervisory agency (e.g., CNBV, CNSF, CONSAR) in Mexico.\n\n\n                                                                             INTERNATIONAL MONETARY FUND             35\n\nMEXICO\n\n\n\n46. CNBV has invested heavily in upgrading its supervisory framework towards a more risk-\nbased supervisory approach. CNBV reshaped the risk-based rating methodology (CEFER) in\nNovember 2015. Supervisory tools have also been standardized and centralized, aiming at\nstreamlining procedures and optimizing resources.\n\n47. Further efforts are needed to strengthen the risk-based supervisory approach. The\nCEFER quantitative methodology to determine inherent risks needs to be simplified. Greater use of\nflexibility and application of expert judgment throughout the supervisory cycle needs to be\nimplemented, as opposed to a formal check-list approach. This will need the set-up of internal\nsupervisory routines to support the development expert judgment among supervisors to better\nassess banks’ risk management practices. There are also important synergies between strengthening\nlegal protection and improving risk-based supervision; the former will allow incentivize supervisors\nto further apply their judgment and discretion to emerging risks.\n\nC. Cybersecurity Regulation and Supervision\n48. Financial digitalization accentuates cyber risk, requiring careful countermeasures.\nInterdependencies within and beyond the financial system make Mexico vulnerable to evolving\ncyber threats. Thus, the CESF has recognized cyber as a risk with the potential to impact financial stability.\n\n49. Banxico and CNBV have made significant progress in enhancing the financial system’s\ncyber resilience but need further enhancement. Banxico has improved its cybersecurity controls,\nincident response framework, threat intelligence function, and security measures of participants that\nconnect to its infrastructure. CNBV has built a dedicated cybersecurity supervision unit and designed\na cyber supervision methodology.\n\n•    Banxico and CNBV need to deepen their cybersecurity strategy for the financial system.\n     The purpose is to specify how to identify, manage, and reduce cyber risk effectively. The cyber\n     strategy should outline how coordination and cooperation occur among public and private\n     stakeholders and other jurisdictions.\n\n•    Cyber risk regulation and supervisory practice need further improvements. CNBV is\n     encouraged to issue regulations or enforceable guidance on Information Communication\n     Technologies (ICT) and cybersecurity to all its supervised financial institutions, not only banks. It\n     could also conduct on-site ICT/cybersecurity inspections and improve off-site supervision. The\n     cyber supervision unit should be given sufficient resources to discharge its responsibilities.\n\n•    Banxico should strengthen the cybersecurity oversight of FMIs. Intensive cybersecurity\n     training of overseers, together with a comprehensive oversight approach and tools and\n     leveraging the cyber strategy, will increase the capabilities and effectiveness of the FMI oversight\n     function. Banxico could also set clear regulatory requirements for all the FMIs under its mandate.\n\n•    Banxico and CNBV would benefit from developing a cyber map of the financial system.\n     Cyber mapping will help identify operational and technological interconnectedness (e.g., critical\n     nodes, transmission channels, and critical service providers).\n\n\n36    INTERNATIONAL MONETARY FUND\n\nMEXICO\n\n\n\n•      Mexico would benefit from improving its public and private platforms for cyber threat\n       intelligence and information sharing. By exchanging cyber information and intelligence,\n       financial entities can improve their defensive capabilities, threat detection techniques, and\n       mitigation strategies. Banxico could work with the financial sector to develop an industry-wide\n       cyber information and intelligence sharing initiative.\n\n•      The authorities could improve the implementation processes around the Bases of\n       Coordination. 13 The Bases of Coordination need to be translated into operational structures,\n       policies, and procedures, with clear leadership by Banxico and CNBV. They could also propose\n       working with the General Attorney’s office to raise awareness of the importance of effective\n       cyber incident investigations and develop guidance for financial entities on how to store, handle,\n       and administer evidence to facilitate investigations.\n\nD. Oversight of Climate-Related Risks\n50. The authorities have taken initial steps to advance the climate agenda, but financial\ninstitutions are generally at an early stage of managing climate-related risks. The CESF\nlaunched the Sustainable Finance Committee to coordinate climate-related policies and green\nfinance practices. Banxico plays an important role in driving the climate risk agenda, for example, by\nactively participating in international fora and building awareness on climate risks in the financial\nsystem. 14 However, financial institutions are at an early stage of integrating climate issues into their\ngovernance frameworks, corporate strategies, risk management, and disclosure practices, reflecting\nthe complexities of assessing these risks and the limited supervisory guidance by regulators.\n\n51. The authorities could lay the ground for integrating climate risks into prudential\nsupervision of banks, insurers, and pension funds. They could leverage the Basel Committee on\nBanking Supervision’s Principles for the Effective Management and Supervision of Climate-related\nFinancial Risks, published in June 2022. Insurers are already required to cover natural catastrophe\nrisk management. Moving forward, CNSF could follow recommendations by the International\nAssociation of Insurance Supervisors to develop further guidance to cover climate risks in\ngovernance, risk management, business strategy and disclosure practices. Similarly, CONSAR has\nissued mandatory guidance for retirement funds and could update it over time with more detailed\nsupervisory guidance.\n\n52. Market transparency should be enhanced by introducing disclosure requirements for\nfirms and investors. The lack of credible climate information is a key barrier to climate risk\nassessment and management and sustainable finance practices in Mexico, as in most other\njurisdictions. To address this challenge, CNBV could move ahead with introducing disclosure\nstandards of climate and ESG information for issuers and asset managers, while including such\ndisclosure for firms in accounting standards.\n\n\n13 The Bases of Coordination is a formal agreement signed by public and private stakeholders to improve coordination and\n\ncooperation within the financial sector in cybersecurity.\n14\n     Banxico created a directorate in charge of these topics in 2021 and published climate risk analysis in recent FSRs.\n\n\n                                                                               INTERNATIONAL MONETARY FUND            37", "excerpt_sha256": "04eee8248e0f99f05600c2c4bd43396dc80cd27f4508cede0d40cabdbb75c473", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": ["The excerpt supports the statements on authority coordination, progress in supervision and cybersecurity, autonomy and resource concerns, consolidated-supervision gaps, and cyber oversight needs."]}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0053-E003", "source_id": "SRC-d29f5588c6484f92", "url": "https://sidof.segob.gob.mx/notas/docFuente/5787691", "title": "Official biosecurity and animal-health strategy document", "publisher": "Diario Oficial de la Federación", "raw_sha256": "ae5ac01fa9c3fe4e319f37d8bf7b63729ead03464958fd195b56bed67e5dd084", "fact_summary": "Biosecurity monitoring indicators show a 2024 baseline of 30% of resolutions and opinions evaluated by independent multidisciplinary technical committees and 40% incorporating systematic expert reviews and international comparisons; INDICADOR\nNombre\nImplementación de monitoreo post-liberación a largo plazo.\nObjetivo\nConsolidar sistemas de monitoreo, trazabilidad, transparencia y participación informada.\nDefinición o\ndescripción\nMide el grado de implementación de sistemas de monitoreo post-liberación de OGM a largo plazo, considerando el diseño y\noperación de programas de vigilancia ambiental y productiva, el establecimiento de indicadores de impacto ecológico, genético y\nsocioeconómico, y el ajuste de medidas de manejo con base en los resultados obtenidos, con el fin de fortalecer la detección\ntemprana y la gestión adaptativa de riesgos.\nDerecho asociado\nDerecho de toda persona a vivir en un medio ambiente adecuado para su alimentación, salud, desarrollo y bienestar\nNivel recolección\nde los datos\nEnero a diciembre\nTendencia esperada\nAscendente\nUnidad responsable de\nreportar el avance\nSECIHTI\nSecretaría Ejecutiva de la CIBIOGEM\nMétodo de cálculo\n(Programas de monitoreo post-liberación operando con indicadores y ajustes de manejo documentados en el año t / Total de\nprogramas de monitoreo previstos en el año t) × 100\nObservaciones\nEl monitoreo deberá 5.2\nELEMENTOS DEL INDICADOR\nNombre\nFortalecimiento de la trazabilidad y gestión de la información.\nObjetivo\nConsolidar sistemas de monitoreo, trazabilidad, transparencia y participación informada.\nDefinición o\ndescripción\nMide el grado de fortalecimiento de la trazabilidad y la gestión de la información en materia de bioseguridad y OGM, considerando la\ncreación de bases de datos integradas sobre OGM y recursos genéticos, la interoperabilidad entre sistemas institucionales y la\nprotección de información sensible y estratégica, garantizando al mismo tiempo el acceso a información pública relevante.\nDerecho asociado\nDerecho de toda persona a vivir en un medio ambiente adecuado para su alimentación, salud, desarrollo y bienestar.\nNivel de\ndesagregación\nNacional\nPeriodicidad o\nfrecuencia de medición\nBienal\nAcumulado o\n\nperiódico\nAcumulado\nDisponibilidad de la\ninformación\nJunio\nUnidad de medida\nPorcentaje\nPeriodo de recolección\nde los datos\nEnero a diciembre\nTendencia esperada\nAscendente\nUnidad responsable de\nreportar el avance\nSECIHTI\nSecretaría Ejecutiva de la CIBIOGEM\nMétodo de cálculo\n(Sistemas de información y bases de datos integradas, interoperables y operando conforme a lineamientos de seguridad en el año t /\nTotal de sistemas previstos en el periodo en el año t) × 100\nObservaciones\nLa protección de la información deberá apegarse a la normatividad en materia de datos sensibles y seguridad de la información, sin\nmenoscabo de las obligaciones de transparencia.\nAPLICACIÓN DEL MÉTODO
